Auspicious and Inauspicious Muhurtas etc. are respectively Agni, Brabma, Gauri, Ganesha, Sarpa, Kartikeya, Suiya (the Sun), Shiva, Durga, Yamaraja, Vishwc Deva, Vishnu, Kamadeva, Shiva and Chandrama (the moon). Notes; On full Amavasya (the 30th Hindi Tithi) the longitude of the sun and that of the moon are the same. At the end of Amavasya the moon, due to her fast movement, goes towards the East from the sun and in this way, the moon's being away from the sun by 12 ', each time there is one lilhi Pratipada etc, that is, when the moon goes away from 0° to 12° from the sun, there is the Pratipada tithi of the Shukla Paksha (the bright half of the month). The rest of the tithis are formed by the moon's going away from the sun by 12° each time. When the distance between the sun and the moon is 180° there is the full disc of the moon in (he sky It is because of this that the 15th tithi is named as 'Poomima' or 'Paumamasi'. After Poornima" (Full moon tithi) when 15 lithis are completed, the dis- tance between the sun and the moon again becomes 0° and this day is the day of Amavasya (full dark tithi). It brings an end of the month In the present Shloka are stated the lords of the lithis. The lords of the tithis in both the Pakshas (fortnights) - the bright half and the dark half - arc the same. The first tithi of the bright half of the month is called Pratipada. In Indian Panchangas (Almanacs) it is indicated by the figure 1 (one) and in the same way the other tithis arc also represented by figures or numbers 1, 2, 3 and so on. Poornima has been allotted the number of 15. The lithis after Poomima arc not rep- resented by the numbers of 16, 17 and so on. they arc again started with Number 1 (one) and arc represented b> numbers I. 2, 3 and so on Only the Amavasya of the month is represented b> the number of 30. Auspicious and Inauspicious Muhurtas etc. are respectively Agni, Brahma, Garni, Ganesha, Saipa, Kartikeya, Surya (the Sun), Shiva, Durga, Yamaraja, Vishwe Deva, Vishnu, Kamadeva, Shiva and Chandrama (the moon). Notes: On full Amavasya (the 30th Hindi Tithi) the longitude of the sun and that of the moon are the same. The Lords or deities of the tithis are as follows; Dates (Tithis) Lords or deities 1. Pratipada Agni (Fire) 2. Dwitiya Brahma (the creator of the universe) 3. Triliya Gauri (Lord Shiva's consort) 4. Chaturthi Gancsha (Parwati's son) 5. Panchmi Sarpa (Serpent, he is also a god) 6. Shashthi Kartikcya (son of Lord Shiva and Parwati) 7, Saptami The Sun (He is also a living god) 8. Ashtami Shiva (the god of destruction) 9. Navami Durga (the goddess who is symbol of pri- meval energy) 10. Dashmi Yama Raja (god of death) . 11. Ekadashi Vishwe Dcva (one of God's names) 12. Dwadashi Vishnu (the god who supports creation) 13. Tryodashi Kamadeva (god of Jove) 14. Chalurdashi Shiva 15. Poomima The moon 30. Amavasya The moon/Manes. The worship, fast and the establishing of the image with life (in Sanskrit hiui wfci'of - Prana Pratishtha) of the gods - these are to be done on the tithis of which they are the Lords or presiding deities. Thus, if one wants to worship Gancsha. it should be done on the 4th tithi the lord or presiding deity of which is Gancsha and so on. Auspicious and Inauspicious Muhurtas •NANDA' ETC. NAMES OF TITHIS AND SIDDHA YOGA: In both the bright half of the month and the dark half of the month, Nanda, Bhadra, Jaya, Rikta and Pooma (in three frequencies) are re- spectively the names of the tithis. The three frequencies of the bright half of the month are respectively inauspicious, medium in auspicious- ncss and hilly auspicious and; the three frequencies of the dark half of the month are regarded respectively fully auspicious, medium in auspiciousness and inauspicious. If, in both the bright and the dark half of the month, the tithis Nanda etc. in order (of all the three fre- quencies) happen to fall on Friday, Wednesday, Tuesday, Saturday and Thursday, then on that day there is the Siddha Yoga. Notes: In the IS tithis of the bright half and the dark half of the month, 1/6/11 are Nandas, 2/7/12 are Bhadras, 3/8/13 are iayas, 4/9/14 are Riktas and 5/10/15 are Poomas (in the Dark half of the month the Poomas are 5/10/30. In the bright half of the month the tithis Pratipada (1), Dwitiya (2), Triliya (3), Chaturthi (4) and Panchmi (5) have been said to be inauspicious. Here inauspiciousness or auspiciousness depends upon the decreasing or the increasing of the moon. The moon disap- pears completely in Ih? night of the Amavasya (30 tithi) and in the nights of the 1/2/3/4/5 tithis she is seen to be slowly increasing. Therefore owing to the weakening of the moon these tithis of the bright half of the month which are of the first frequency are said to be inauspicious. The second frequency of the tithis of the bright half of the month, that is 6/7/8/9/10 witness increase in the kalas of the moon, The strength of the moon increases to a certain degree. There- fore. these tithis are neither auspicious nor inauspicious or I hey arc medium in auspiciousness. In the 3rd frequency of the tithis of the bright half of the month, that is 11/12/13/14/15 the moon is endowed with all the kalas and therefore these tithis have been spoken of as auspicious. On the other hand in the dark half of the month in the first frequency of the tithis, that is, 1/2/3/4/5 the moon decreases slowly. But she is seen in her greater number of kalas. Therefore the tithis of the first frequency of the dark half of the month that is 1/2/3/4/5 have been said to be fully auspicious. In the 2nd frequency of the tithis of the dark half of the month 6/7/8/9/10 the moon gradually decreases in strength, and so these tithis have been said to be medi- um in auspiciousness. And in the 3rd frequency of the tithis of the Auspicious and Inauspicious Muhurtas •NANDA' ETC. Muhjfla Chinta Mani dark half of the month, the moon's being completely devoid of strength, therefore these tithis 11/12/13/14/15 have been said to be inauspicious. If the Nan da tithis (1/6/11) happen to fall on Friday. Bhadra tithis (2/7/12) on Wednesday. Jaya tithis (3/8/13) on Tuesday, Rikta tithis (4/9/14) on Saturday and Poorna tithis (5/10/15) on Thursday, then on that day there happens to be Siddha Yoga. This Yoga is counted among the most auspicious yogas and it is regarded as suc- cess giving in one's enforts. Brahma rishi Vashistha has said that as the Ekadashi titlu (II) destroys all the sins if on this tithi one keeps fast, gives alms, per- forms worship, listens to the religious stories, performs the comple- tion rites of some fast 'viriim' etc., in the same way the above men- tioned Siddha tithis confer auspicious effects by destroying the de- fects relating to Muhurtas such as those of Masa Shunya (zero month), Masa Dagdha (burnt month). Inauspicious day such as the day of Saturday happening to fall on the inauspicious tiths of Rikta becomes a day of Siddha Yoga and confers auspicious effects The Shloka in Tabular form Name of Tithis Days Yoga Nanda i Friday Bhadra Wcdncsdm Siddha Java Tucsdax Yoga Rikta Saturday Poorna Thursdat In the bright half of the month Inauspi- cious Medium in auspi- ciousncss Auspici- ous In the dark half of the month auspici- ous Mrdium in auspi- ciousncss Inauspici- ous ' dark half of the month, the moon's being completely devoid of strength, therefore these tithis 11/12/13/14/15 have been said to be inauspicious. Auspicious and Inauspicious Muhunas KARKACHA AND SAMVARTAKA YOGA; If Shashthi etc tithis in right order of tithis fall on Saturday etc in reverse order of days, there is Adhama Yoga (Krakacha) on these days. There is Adhama (Samvartaka) yoga also if Pratipada (1) falls on Wednesday and Saptami <7) falls on Sunday. And teeth should not be cleaned with Neern slick etc on the Shashthi (6) etc. Tithis. Notes; If it happens that the Shashthi (6) tithi falls on Satur- day, Saptami (7) on Friday. Astami (8) on Thursday, Navami (9) on Wednesday, Dashmi (10) on Tuesday, Ekadashi (11) on Monday and Dwadashi (12), on Sunday, the yoga formed on these days is not aus- picious and good. Shri Govind ji (the writer's brother) has given this yoga the name of 'Karkacha yoga'. And the yoga that is formed if Pratipada (1) happens to fall on Wednesday and Saptami (7) on Sun- day has been given the name of 'Samwartaka'. Teeth are not to be cleaned with stick of any tree (such as Neem tree) in these yogas. According to 'Kashi Khanda Granatha' collection of wood should not be made on Pratipada (1), Amavasya (30), Shashthi (6) and Navami (9) and on Sunday 11 (3 11 On Shashthi (6). Muhurta Chmta Mam Vikram Samvat begins front the first tithi of Chaitra Shukla (Shukla Pratipada) according to the Chaitradi system, and the Vikrama Samvat started from 57 B.C. The Lunar month which begins (on the Pratipada of the bright half of the month) when the Sun is in the sign of Pisces and which ends (on the Amant or Amavasya) when the Sun is in the sign of Aries is called the 'Shuddha' (pure) Chaitra (March) Masa or month. The names of pure months are as follows - 1. Chaitra 5. Shravana 9 Marga Shirsa 2. Vaisakha 6. Bhadrapada 10. Pausa 3. Jyeshtha 7. Asvina 11. Magha 4. Ashadha 8. Kartika 12. Phalguna As long as the number of Aries Sankranti etc in a Solar year (12 Solar months) is equal to the number of Amanta (Amavasya), the months are considered to be pure months. When in a year the number of Amantas becomes greater than the number of Sankrantis, the additional month is called 'the extra' month or 'Mala' month. Similarly, when in a Solar year the amanta number becomes fewer than the Sankranti number, the one month which is fewer is called the 'Kshaya' month. The difference between the two sunrises is called one savana day. 30 Savana days make one Savana month and 12 Savana months make one Savana year. The time between the rise of a Nakshua or star in the horizon and another rise of it on the next day is called one Sidereal day . 30 such Sidereal days make one Sidereal month and 12 Sidereal months make one Sidereal year. In this, book the 'amanta' or tht Sukhladi system has been re- garded as the base of calculation Where some other system wilt be used, it will be separately indicated cfaTTf^RTWf fiKteqi | | ^ | | sr^icn fEnffajin' cmi i Muhurta Chmta Mam Vikram Samvat begins from the first tithi of Chaitra Shukla (Shukla Pratipada) according to the Chaitradi system, and the Vikrama Samvat started from 57 B.C. Muhurta Chinte Mam H^rf 11 11 COMBINATIONS WHICH ARE TO BE LEFT OUT IN DO- ING ALL AUSPICIOUS DEEDS; The Panchami Tithi (5) with the Hasta Nakshtra falling on Sunday, Saptami (7) with Ashwini on Tues- day, Shashthi (6) with Mrigasira on Monday, Ashtami (8) with Anuradha on Wednesday, Dashmi (10) with Rewati on Friday, Navami (9) with Pushya on Thursday and Ekadashi (11) with Rohini on Saturday-these combinations are to be avoided in doing all auspi- cious deeds Notes; If there is the conjunction of only the Nakshtra and the day, it is auspicious and should be adopted in doing all auspicious deeds; but it becomes inauspicious if there is the conjunction of the above stated tithis. This yoga is referred to as Madhu Sarpisha yoga Maharishi Vashistha has spoken of another kind of Madhu Sarpisha yoga which is named as 'Halahala' yoga. According to Vashistha the conjunction of Sunday-Panchatni-Kritika, Monday-Dwitiya Chitra, Tuesday-Poornima-Rohini, Wcdncsday-Saptami-Bharani, Thursday- Tray odashi-Anuradha and Saturday-Ashtami-Rewati-makes what is called the inauspicious yoga of Halahala. "arzmi tort xf i ^ 119? 11 YOGAS (COMBINATIONS) WHICH ARE TO BE AVOIDED IN ENTERING NEW HOUSE. TRAVELLING AND MARRYING The rise of Ashw ini Nakshtra on Tuesday, of Rohini on Saturday and Pushya Nakshtra on Thursday-these combinations are to be avoided respectively for entering newly constructed House, travelling and mar- rying Notes. Although siddhi yoga that is. the yoga which ensures, the successful completion of a deed, is formed in the conjunction of the above-mentioned days and Nakshtras. but this yoga is to be re- jected in the ceremony of entering a newly constructed house, trav- elling and marrying. Muhurta Chinla Mani from Mrigshira to the Nakshtra on Monday. If the Nakshtra on Mon- day is Mrigshira itself, then the yoga on this day will be the 'Ananda' as counting from Mrigshira to Mrigshira gives I. If there is the rise of Aridra on Monday, the yoga formed on this day will be Kala- Danda, if of Punarvasu, the yoga formed on this day will be Dhoomra. Other yogas will be formed in the same way due to the rise of different Nakshtras on Monday. The 28 yogas will be formed on all the Seven days of the week due to the rise of different 28 Nakshtras on these days. Here is an example for more clarification, Suppose there is the rise of the Vishakha Nakshtra on Thursday. To know what will be the yoga on this day we shall count from the Anuradha Nakshtra to the Vishakha Nakshtra. The number comes to be 28. Therefore, the conjunction of Vishakha Nakshtra and Thursday will form the 28lh yoga which is Pravardhamana yoga, REJECTION OR LEAVING OUT OF INAUSPICIOUS YOGAS: The first 5 Ghslis in the Dhwanksha, the Vajra and the Mudgar yogas, the first 4 Ghatis in the Padma and the Lumba yogas, the first 7 Ghatis in the Gada, the first ghati in the Dhumra, the first two Ghatis in the Musala arc rejected as inauspicious And the full lime of Rakshasa. THINGS TO BE REJECTED IN DOING ALL THE AUSPI- CIOUS DEEDS: The Lagna or the Ascendant and the Navamsha oc- cupied by the Moon and the malefic planets (the emaciated Moon, the Sun, Mars, and Saturn), the third part of a Ghati or 20 Palas (4 minutes) before and after mid day and mid night-these are to be re- jected in doing all the auspicious deeds. Similarly, the Navamsha of a malefic planet, three days before an eclipse, seven days after a por- tent (like an earthquake etc.) and an eclipse and the whole day on which an auspicious portent occurs—these should also be avoided in doing, all auspicious deeds. And the Nakshtra cleaved by planets, the Nakshtra in which there is the planetary war and the Nakshtra in which there is some Utpaata or portent—these three Nakshtras are to be rejected for six months in doing all the auspicious deeds. Notes: The Sun, Mars, Saturn, Raho, Ketu, emaciated Moon and Mercury together with a malefic planet, are regarded as malefic planets. Reversal in natural events is called a portent. Portents are of three types: 1. Celestial Portents, 2. Atmospheric Portents, and 3. Terrestrial Portents. In Shloka 4S5 Chapter 46 of 'Brihat Samhita' the writer speaks of portents in these words; "The celestial portents are the unnatural behaviour of planets and asterisms, meteors, thunders, storms and ha- los; the atmospheric ones are arcrial cities, rainbows etc and; the ter- restrial ones arise from the moving and the Stationery objects. Those portents which are generated by the peculiar nature of the seasons and do not lead to evil consequences are called auspi- cious portents. According to 'Brihat Samhita' the auspicious portents in different seasons are as follows; I. Spring (Basant) seasons (Constituted by the Lunar months of Chaitra and Vaishaka or roughly March and April): Thunder-bolts, lightning, earth tremor, bright glare at twilight, sounds of loud thun- der, halos, dust, smoke in forests, the Sun's being crimson at the lime of rising and selling, the exudation of food, sweet juice, oil and an THINGS TO BE REJECTED IN DOING ALL THE AUSPI- CIOUS DEEDS: The Lagna or the Ascendant and the Navamsha oc- cupied by the Moon and the malefic planets (the emaciated Moon, the Sun, Mars, and Saturn), the third part of a Ghati or 20 PaJas (4 minutes) before and after mid day and mid night-these are to be re- jected in doing all the auspicious deeds. Auspicious and Inauspicious Muhurtas over-abundant yield of flowers and fruits on trees and increased sen- suality on the part of bulls and birds. 2. Summer (Grecsham) season (constituted by Jyeshtha and Ashadha, roughly May and June): The sky tarnished by shooting stars and falling meteors, the orbs of the luminaries (the Sun and the Moon) being tawny, blaze and explosion without fire, smoke dust and strong wind filling the sky, the twilight red like red lotus, the sky looking like the ocean with surging waves and the drying up of the waters of the river. 3. Rainy (Varsha) season (constituted by Shravana and Bhado, roughly July and August); Rainbow halos, lightning, sprouting of withered trees, tremor, upheaval, unnatural appearances, rumbling and splitting of the earth, rising, flowing upwards and flooding of lakes, rivers and wells respectively and falling off of mountains and houses 4. Autumn (sharad) season (constituted by Ashwina and Kartika, roughly September and October). The sight of celestial damsels, spir- its, Gand harvas, aerial Chariots, marvellous things, and of planets, stars and constellations in the day time in the sky, sounds of singing and musical instruments in the forests and on mountain plateaus, in- crease of crops and diminution of water. 5. DEWY (Hemant) season (constituted by Marga shirsha and Pausha, roughly November and December): Cold wind, frost, cries of birds and beasts, sight of demons yakshas and such other invis- ible beings, aerial voices being heard, the quarters along with the sky, forests and mountains getting darkened with smoke and the high ris- ing and setting of the Sun. 6. Winter (Shishira) Season (Constituted by Magba and Phalguna, roughly January and February): Snow fall, uonalural winds, appearance erf deformed and super human beings, the sky looking like dark coUymm, and colourful with the fall of stars and meteors, wom- en, cows, goats horses, beasts and birds giving birth to freakish young oks and unnaiura] changes in lea-res, sproirfs and creepers. In the countries or regions in which these auspicious portents happen naturally, where these happen daily, they cannot be called portents. Thus in the hilly countries or regions the falling of snow should not be called an auspicious portent. ##### PDF PAGE 27 #####

Muhurta Chinta Man! sphere, the celestial latitude of a planet gets in the same direction with the celestial latitude of a Nakshtra, the planet is said to be cleav- ing that Nakshtra. Such a Nakshtra is also to be rejected. Planetary war: When the planets move in the sky along their orbits lying over one another, they appear to our eyes to move on one surface or plane as a result of their great distance from the earth. According to the degree of their seeming approachment there are four kinds of war. 1. Bhcda (occuitation, cleaving) 2. Ullekha (grazing) 3. Amsumardana (clashing of the rays) 4, Apsavya (passing south ward). When two planets appear to have a single orb on account of their apparent over lapping the fight is called Bheda. In this the orb of the lower planet abstracts completely the upper one. When the rims of the two orbs graze each other, it is called Ullekha. When Use rays of the two planets situated closely appear to be clashing with one another it is Amsumardana. When a planet covering another moves towards the south it is Apasavya ^^5 1*1 •^TRTf^ | THE NAKSHTRAS IN WHICH ECLIPSES OCCUR; The Nakshtra in which there is the full eclipse of the Sun is to be reject- ed for six months, for 3 months if there is the half eclipse, for one month if there is the one fourth part eclipse. If the eclipsed Sun or Moon sets then three days before the eclipse, and if the eclipsed Sun or Moon rises then three days after it, no auspicious deed is to be done. And if there is the half eclipse at other time than the lime of rising or setting then 3 days before and 3 days after and the full day of the eclipse, should be avoided for doing all auspicious deeds ORGANS OF THE PANCHANGA WHICH ARE TO BE AVOIDED IN DOING AUSPICIOUS DEEDS' The Nakshtra at the time of bath, the month of the birth, the dale of the birth. DETAILS OF BHADRA: In the Shukla Paksha or the bright half of the month there is Bhadra on the 8th and ISth tithis in the first half part of the tithi; and Bhadra is there also on the 4th and the 11th tithis in the latter half part of these tithis. In 'Ratna kosha" there arc given the names of the Bhadta - Karana and the effects are similar to the names These names respec- tively are: (1) Hansi (2) Nandini (3) Trishira (4) Sunuskhi (5) Karalika (6) Vaikriti (7) Raudramukhi and (8) Chaturmuklu. The names of Bhadra are to be understood in this order. Auspicious and Inauspicious Muhurlas er signs except those of Taurus, Aries, Pisces and Aquarius, and if after becoming retrograde he does not move back to the former sign (the sign in which he has become Atichari) then that Varsha (the Guru Varsha related to the former sign which has been crossed tte to his being Atichari) will be called Lupta-Sanwatsar (or Mataatichaar). This Lupta samvatsar is to be completely rejected in performing aus- picious deeds. Notes: The Bhoga Kaala (the traversed period) of Jupiter of me sign when he moves with his medium speed is called Samvatsar. These, by the names of Prabhava etc, are the Guru Varshas or Guru years. If Jupiter with his Spashta or true gati (speed) moves into one sign in every year then they are the Shuddha Varshas (pure years) related to their respective signs. According to the above Shloka if Jupiter becomes Atichari in the signs of Taurus, Aries Aquarius and Pisces and being retrograde docs not move back to his former sign, and in this way causes a Lupta Samvatsar, that Lupta Samvatsar is not to be rejected in do- ing auspicious deeds. Atichaar: The reaching of a planet to a certain place before its fixed time, that is, the time in which hs would have reached with his natural speed, is called Atichaar. Therefore if a planet enters a certain sign before its normal time of entering it with his medium speed, then it will be called the Atichaar of thai planet. This Atichaar is not regarded as auspicious. The Atichaars particularly of Jupiter, the Sun and the Moon are inauspicious-effects-giving because the Atichaar of the Moon causes Kshaya tithi (non-occurrence of a tithi), that of the Sun causes Kshaya Maas (non-occurrence of a month), and that of Jupiter causes Kshaya Varsha (non occurrence of a year). If Jupiter moves with his natural or medium speed his time of moving in one sign is one year or one Samv atsar. These Varshas (years) which are named as Prabhava etc. are called Guru Varshas (or Jupiter years). If the moving time of Jupiter in one sign is one year then that year is the Shuddha Varsha (pure ycari related to that sign. Contrary to this, if he moves m mo signs in one year then such a Jupiter, became of his not completing one year with his natural or medium speed, is called Alichaari. If such a Jupiter does not beoonse retrograde and does not come back to his former sign, then the Varsha or year of the former sign w ill be called Lupta-Samvatsar or 4! I ^ 11 VAAR PRAVARTI (THE TIME AT WHICH A DAY BE- GINS: One fourth (1/4) is to be obtained of the distance (in yojanas; a yojana is a measure of distance) of one's own city from the cities or the regions through which the Prime meridian line has passed (Ac- cording to the ancient sages the cities and the regions through which it has passed are Kalicut, Sholapur, Ujjain, Kumkshetra, Jalagaon, Indore, Sawai Madhopur, Jaipur, Pilani, Ludhtana, iammu etc), wheth- er one's own city is situated to the west or to the east of this line. This one fourth part is to be subtracted from the number of these very yojanas. The figure which is arrived at after subtraction is sup- posed to be so many pafas and these pal as are added to IS ghatis (If one's own city is to the west of the Prime Meridian line, then these Palas are added to 15 ghatis; if it is to the east then these are to be subtracted from 15 ghatis. How much less the number of the ghatis and palas obtained in this way is from the Dinaardha (Half of the Dinmaana) of one's own city that much number of ghatis and palas will be the Vaar Pravarti alter the rising of the Sun. If the num- ber is greater, then Vaar Pravarti will be as much before the rising of the Sun as this number is greater than the Dinaardha of one's own city Notes Here is an example to know the Vaarpravarti. Suppose; Varanasi is ai a distance of 63 yojanas (I yojana = 5 kosha and one kosha = aboul I miles) from the Prime Meridian Line, One fourth part of it will be 63 ^ 4 = 15"'- 45. This figure when it is subtracted from 63 gives V - 15 These are palas and these are subtracted from 15 ghatis. The remainder comes to be 14/12/45 Then suppose m a Muhurta Chinia Mam Viluptabd. These are called Swami Tithyamsha. A Tithyamsha is equal to the 15th part of Dinmaan and is considered to be the part of the Nakshtra The consideration of the defect of Vaar Shoola and Nakshtra Shoola is also to be made both in the Hora and in the Muhurta; and Parighdanda (the special defect of the time of a Journey) according to Sbloka 36 of Chapter 11) is not to be violated even in the Muhurta of the Nakshtra. Notes; A particular deed is to be performed on that very day on which it has been advised to be done according to the Shastras or classics on Astrology. THE MUHURTAS WITH REGARD TO DIGGING WELLS OR TANKS AND BEGINNING DANCING; In the Nakshtras of Amiiadha, Hasta, those called Dhruwa, and thoK of Dhanistha, Shatbhisha, Magha, Poorvashadha, Revati Pushya and Mrigshira; if there is Metcury or Jupiter in the Ascendant; if the Matefics are weak; if Venus is situated in the 10th House from the Ascendant and the Moon is of the watery signs (9/10/11/12), in such a Muhmta the dig- ging of a well and a tank etc is auspicious. In the above said Nakshtras excluding Poorvashadha and Magha and including Jyeshtha Nakshtra, if there is Meicuiy in the Ascendant and the Moon is there in Gemini or Virgo sign and there are benefic planets in the 4th House, then the learning or the first beginning of Dancing is auspi- cious. Notes; In the above mentioned Shloka the situation of Mercu- ry in the Ascendant, that of the Moon in the Gemini or the Virgo sign and then that of benefic planets in the 4th House have been spo- ken of as auspicious. Now, after fee situation of thw two benefic planets (Mercury and the Moon) only two benefic planets remain. Therefore, from the phrase in the Shloka the plural of a benefic planet does not seem to be proper due to the reason that possibly there may be one or two benefic planets in the 4th House. Therefore this reading may be proper. Although according to other teamed astrologers the Nakshtras auspicious for digging small well, well and tank are different, even then the Achaiya (the writer of the present book) has spoken of Nakshtras of the same type for all of them because of the same kind of all the three (they are all reservoir of water) things. THE NAKSHTRAS OF VAAPI OR SMALL WELL; Swati, Ashwini, Pushya, Hasta, Punarvasu, Rewati and Shatabhisha. THE NAKSHTRAS OF DIGGING WELL: Hasta, Pushya, Dhanistha, Shatabhisha. Anuradha. Magha and all the three Uttaras THE NAKSHTRAS OF BUILDING TANK: Ashwini, Mrigshira, Rewati, all the 3 Uttaras, Rohini Pushya, Shatbhisha, and Poorvashadha. 11 ^ 11 THE MUHURTAS WITH REGARD TO DIGGING WELLS OR TANKS AND BEGINNING DANCING: In the Nakshtras of Anuradha, Hasta, those called Dhruwa, and those of Dhanistha, Shatbhisha, Magha, Poorvashadha, Revati Pushya and Mrigshira; if there is Mercury or Jupiter in the Ascendant; if the Malefics are weak; if Venus is situated in the 10th House from the Ascendant and the Moc»i is of the watery signs (9/10/11/12), in such a Muhmta the dig- ging of a well and a tank etc is auspicious. In the above said Nakshtras excluding Poorvashadha and Magha and including Jyeshtha Nakshtra, if there is Mercury in the Ascendant and the Moon is there in Gemini or Virgo sign and there are benefic planets in the 4th House, then the learning or the first beginning of Dancing is auspi- cious. Notes: In the above mentioned SUoka the situation of Mercu- ry in the Ascendant, that of the Moon in the Gemini or the Virgo sign and then that of benefic planets in the 4th House have been spo- ken of as auspicious. Now, after the situation of these two benefic planets (Mercury and the Moon) only two benefic planets remain. Therefore, from the phrase in the Shloka the plural of a benefic planet does not seem to be proper due to the reason that possibly there may be one or two benefic planets in the 4th House. Therefore this reading may be proper. Although according to other teamed astrologers tie Nakshtras auspicious for digging small well, well and tank are different, even then the Acharya (the writer of the present book) has spoken of Nakshtras of the same type for all of them because of the same kind of all the three (they are all reservoir of water) things. THE NAKSHTRAS OF VAAPI OR SMALL WELL: Swati, Ashwini, Pushya, Hasta, Punarvasu, Rewati and Shatabhisha THE NAKSHTRAS OF DIGGING WELL: Hasta, Pushya, Dhanistha, Shatabhisha. Anuradha, Magha and all the three Uttaras. THE NAKSHTRAS OF BUILDING TANK: Ashwini, Mrigshira, Rewati, all the 3 Uttaras, Rohini Pushya, Shatbhisha, and Poorvashadha.

PERFORMANCE OF RELIGIOUS RITES OR DEEDS: the per- formance of religious rites is proper and auspicious in the Nakshtras called Milra (friend)-Mrigshira, Rewati, Chitra, and Anuradha-those called Kshipra-Hasta, Ashwini and Pushya-those called Chara or Mov- able-Swati, Punarvasu, Shravana, Dhanishtha, Shatbhisa-those called Dhruwa-U ttaraphalguni, Uttarashadha, Uttara Bhadrapada and Rohini- on Sunday and on the days of Bcncfic Planets, that is, on Wednes- days, Thursdays, Fridays and Mondays, if there is the Shadavarga of Mercury or Jupiter, in the Ascendant or if there is Jupiter in the As- cendant and Jupiter is strong also. Notes: The Sanskrit interpreter of 'Muhurta Chintmani', Shri Govind has regarded Anuradha as the Nakshtra called Mitra (friend) because this Nakshtra has 'Mitra' as one of its names. If we accept this statement, then only 13 Nakshtras will be accepted as proper in which religious rites may be performed and then the Nakshtras of Mrigshira, Rewati and Chitra will not be accepted The Natal Ascendant, Saptamamsha, Decanate, Navamsha, Dwadashamsha and Trimshamsha have been called as Shadavarga. When there is the sign of Mercury or of Jupiter in these shadavargas it should be understood that there is the shadavarga of Mercury or of Jupiter in the Ascendant. If there is the sign of these planets in the Shadavarga it should be regarded as auspicious. Muhurta Chintm Mam pacification of which 'Nimitta' Shanti or pacification is arranged to be done, it is not necessary that one should consult the Muhurta for arranging the performance of the Nimitta Shanti. ^f*TTfr3rf^ ^f^pnT^Tcr: 1 AVI 11^11 THE MUHURTA FOR DOING HAVANA OR YAJNA (BURNING SACRED FIRE): In the three Nakshtras each from the Nakshtra of the Sun to that of the Moon, the Planets respectively of the Sun, Mercury, Venus, Saturn, Moon, Mars, Jupiter, Rahu and Ketu are the Sharers of the ahuti of the Havana (Ahuli—the spices etc that are poured into fire in one led). The Abuti of the Havana is inauspi- cious in the Nakshtra which belongs to a Malefic Planet Notes: Tire Ahuti which is given in the 3 Nakshtras from the Nakshtra in which the Sun is there will be received by the Sun; in the 3 Nakshtras after that by Mercury, then in the 3 Nakshtras alter that by Venus, then in the 3 Nakshtras after that by Saturn, then in the 3 Nakshtras after that by the Moon, then in the 3 Nakshtras af- ter that by Mars, then in the 3 Nakshtras after that by Jupiter, then in the 3 Nakshtras after that by Rahu and lastly in the 3 Nakshtras after that by Ketu. Suppose on the day on which we have to do Havana or Yajna the Sun is there in the Nakshtra of Rewati and the Moon is placed in that of Poonarvasu. The Number of Nakshtras from Rewati to Poonarvasu is 8. If 8 is divided by 3, the remainder is 2 (8^-3 = 2). The meaning of 2 as remainder is that the Planet coming after 2 plan- ets, that is, that coming after the Sun and Mercury will be the claim- ant of the 'Ahuli'. The planet after these 2 planets is Venus and she will claim this 'Ahuti'. This fact may be stated in this way also that on the day on which 'Havana' or 'Yajna' is perfomsed, the First 'Ahuti' in the 'Havana' will go into the mruuh of Venus or will be got by Venus. Venus is a Eterefic Planet, therefore the effect will be beneficial. If by chance the Abuti is received by a Malefic Planet, then 'Shanti Karma' or 'Pacification rite' should be pcrfornred and a cow should be given in charity to a Brahmin who is of good con- duct and who is engaged in penance. Muhurta Chmta Mam paciiication of which 'Nimitta' Shanti or pacification is arranged to be done, it is not necessary that one should consult the Muhurta for arranging the performance of the Nimitta Shanti. ^f*TTfr3Ff^r*i ^ffoWsfnTTTcj: | ^ 11^11 THE MUHURTA FOR DOING HAVANA OR YAJNA (BURNING SACRED FIRE): In the three Nakshtras each from the Nakshtra of the Sun to that of the Moon, the Planets respectively of the Sun, Mercury, Venus, Saturn, Moon, Mars, Jupiter, Rahu and Ketu are the Sharers of the ahuti of the Havana (Ahuli—the spices etc that are poured into fire in one lot). The Ahuti of the Havana is inauspi- cious in the Nakshtra which belongs to a Malefic Planet Notes: Tire Ahuti which is given in the 3 Nakshtras from the Nakshtra in which the Sun is there will be received by the Sun; in the 3 Nakshtras after that by Mercury, then in the 3 Nakshtras after that by Venus, then in the 3 Nakshtras after that by Saturn, then in the 3 Nakshtras after that by the Moon, then in the 3 Nakshtras af- ter that by Mars, then in the 3 Nakshtras after that by Jupiter, then in the 3 Nakshtras after that by Rahu and lastly in the 3 Nakshtras after that by Ketu. Suppose on the day on which we have to do Havana or Yajna the Sun is there in the Nakshtra of Rewati and the Moon is placed in that of Poonarvasu. The Number of Nakshtras from Rewati to Poonarvasu is 8. If 8 is divided by 3, the remainder is 2 (8 -5- 3 = 2). The meaning of 2 as remainder is that the Planet ooming after 2 plan- ets, that is, that coining after the Sun and Mercury will be the claim- ant of the 'Ahuti'. The planet after these 2 planets is Venus and she will claim this 'Ahuti'. This fact may be stated in this way also that on the day on which 'Havana' or 'Yajna' is performed, the First 'Ahuti' in the 'Havana' will go into the mmuh of Venus or will be got by Venus. Venus is a Beaefic Planet, therefore the effect will be beneficial If by chance the Ahuti is received by a Malefic Planet, then 'Shanti Karma' or 'Pacification rite' should be performed and a cow should be given in charity to a Brahmin who is of good con- duct and who is engaged in penance.


##### PDF PAGE 74 #####

According to Maharishi Kashyap there is the obtainment sf fe® following effects in the event of the falling of the 'Ahuti' in the mouth of the various planets. When Havana or yajna is performed, if the Son receives the 'Ahuti' there is the destruction of the Auspicious effects; if the Moon receives it the crops will be inundated; if Mars is the receiver there will be the fear of fire; if Mercury, the increase in the intellectual powers; if Jupiter, the fulfilment of the desired objective; if Venus, there is the profit of the worldly and the other worldly property or wealth; if Saturn, there is the destruction of wealth and the loss of kingdom; if Rahu. there will be loss of every kind and; if Ketu there will be famine, death and loss of kingdom. ilk 113511 THE ABODE OF LIVING OF FERE IN THE HAVANA AND THE AUSPICIOUS AND INAUSPICIOUS EFFECTS OF IT: Ow should be added to the 'Tithi' (the Tshta Tithi or the desired Titht from the Shukla Pratipada or the I Tithi of the Shukla Paksha) and then the number of the day (the number from Sunday) is to be added to it and the Sum is to be divided by 4. If the reminder is 3 or~Or then the abode of the Fire is on the Earth and by doing the Havana on that day the performer gets happiness; if the remainder comes to be 1, the abode of the Fire is in Heaven and by doing Havana on that day there is the loss of life; if the remainder comes to be 2, the abode of the Fire is in the Paalal or the Hades (the underworld) and by doing Havana on that day there is the loss of wealth. Notes: Here the number of the Tithi is to be counted from the Pratipada or the first tithi of a Hindi month in the Shukia Paksha or the Bright half of the Month. Sashthi Tithi (6) of the Krishna Paksha of the month of Kartika on Thursday, then 15 is to be added to 6 which gives 6 + 15 = 21. Thus 2] is the real Tithi. 1 is added to it which gives 22. the day of Thursday which is 5 when counted from Sunday is to be added to 22 and the resultant figure comes to be 27. Then 27 is to be divided by 4, the remainder is 3. According hi the Shloka 1 is added to the Sum of the Tilhi and the day and the resultant figure is to be divided by 4. Then tf the resnainder comes to be 1, the abode of fire is supposed to be in Heaven, if it comes to be 2, in the Hades or the underworld and if it comes to be 3 or 0, on the Earth. In this way the abode of fire remains only in 3 places, that is the abode of fire is there only in Heaven, in the Hades or the underworld (Pataal) and on the Earth. Therefore, the figure could be divided by 3 only and there was also not the necessity of adding one (1) to the sum of the Tithi and the day. Bhaskaracharya has clearly said that 12 yojanas (96 miles) above the Earth there is the atmosphere of the Earth; the clouds etc are in this atmosphere The real abode of the fire is in the order of Hades-Earth-Sky (clouds) and Heaven. Therefore after dividing the Sum of the Tithi and the day by 4 due to the remainder as 1 the abode of the fire should be in the Hades or the underworld, as 2, on the earth, as 3 in the sky (clouds) and as 0, in Heaven. But due to Heavens being the best abode, the Acharyas or the learned kept this order of the abode of Fire-Heaven-Hades or the underworld-the Earth and the sky (clouds). It was due to this fact that there arose the necessity of adding 1 to the Sum of the Tithi and the Day. Therefore, due to the remainder being 1 the abode of the fire was supposed to be in Heaven; it be- ing 2, in the Hades or the underworld; as the Earth and the Sky (clouds) are supposed as one, so due to the remainder being 3 and 0, the abode of fire was supposed to be on the Earth. f^TT 11^(9 11 THE MUHURTA FOR EATING THE NEW GRAINS; The eat- ing of the new grains is auspicious in the Nakshtras named as 'Chara' or Movable (Swati, Poonarvasu. Such tests are not given in the present times. As regards the 'Nadi Nakshtras'. Shrimad Govinda ji has said in the Sanskrit commentary of this book (Muhurta ChinatmanT); "For the ordinary human beings the First Nakshlra from their Janama or Birth Nakshtia is called the 'Janama Nakshtra; the 10th, 'Karma Nakshtra'; the 16th "Sanghatika Nakshlra; the 18th, 'Samudaya Nakshtra'; the 23rd, 'Vinasha Nakshtra'; and the 25th, 'Manasa Nakshtra';". For the kings, besides the above 6 Nakshtras, the other Nakshtras such as the Nakshtra of the caste, of the country (region) and of the time of their Abhishcka or Coronation, are also consid- ered. In this way, for them there are 9 'Nadi Nakshtras". The Nakshtra of the country (region) is taken as per the name of that country. The Nakshtra prevailing at the time of 'Rajyabhisheka' or coronation 1$ referred to as 'Abhisheka' Nakshtra. The Nakshtras of the castes arc as follows; 1. The Nakshtras of the Brahmins; Poorva Phalgunt, Poorvashadha, Poorva Bhadrapada and Kntika. 2. The Nakshtras of the Kshlnyas (Martial Races): Ultara Phalguni. Uttarashadha, Uttarabhadrapada, and Pushya 3. The Nakshtras of the farmers: Rewati, Anuradha. Magha and Rohini. 4. The Nakshtras of the Vaishyas Punarvasu. Hasla. Abhijit and Ashwini. 5. The Nakshtras of the criminal castes Moola Ardra, Swati and Shatabhisha 6 The Nakshtras of the Sbudras. Mngasfaira, Jyeshtha, Chitra and Dhamshtha 7 The Nakshtras of the Chandala Caste (the caste the mem- bers of which lake toll for cremation) Ashicsha. Vishakha, Shravana and Qharani The abo\e mentioned 7 were the castes in which the Indian Community was divided in those days when the book was written Muhurta Chinta Mam doing that deed the metal burnt his hand or the part of the body which touched the metal. The innocent did not get any harm from the metal. Such tests are not given in the present times. As regards the 'Nadi Nakshtras'. Shrimad Govinda ji has said in the Sanskrit commentary of this book (Muhurta ChinatmanT): 'Tor the ordinary human beings the First Nakshtra from their Janama or Birth Nakshtra is called the 'Janama Nakshtra; the 10th, 'Karma Nakshtra'; the 16th "Sanghatika Nakshtra; the 18th, 'Samudaya Nakshtra'; the 23rd, 'Vinasha Nakshtra'; and the 25th, 'Manasa Nakshtra';". For the kings, besides the above 6 Nakshtras, the other Nakshtras such as the Nakshtra of the caste, of the country (region) and of the time of their Abhisheka or Coronation, are also consid- ered. In this way, for them there are 9 'Nadi Nakshtras". The Nakshtra of the country (region) is taken as per the name of that country. The Nakshtra prevailing at the time of 'Rajyabhisheka' or coronation is referred to as 'Abhisheka' Nakshtra. The Nakshtras of the castes arc as follows: 1. The Nakshtras of the Brahmins: Poorva Phalgunt, Poorvashadha, Poorva Bhadrapada and Kniika. 2. The Nakshtras of the Kshtriyas (Martial Races): Uttara Phalguni. Uttarashadha. Uttarabhadrapada, and Pushya 3. The Nakshtras of the fanners: Rewati, Anuradha. Magha and Rohini. 4. The Nakshtras of the Vaishyas Punarvasu. Hasta. Abhijit and Ashwini. 5 The Nakshtras of the criminal castes: Moola Ardra, Swat) and Shatabhisha 6 The Nakshtras of the Shudras Mngashira, Jyeshtha. Chitra and Dhamshtha 7 The Nakshtras of the Chandala Caste (the caste the mem- bers of which take toll for cremation) Ashlesha, Vishakha, Shravana and Bharani The above mentioned 7 were the castes in which the Indian Community was dwided in those days when the book was written

The odd days after the appearance of menses should also be left out for sexual intercourse which is meant for conception and for having a son. Sunday and Tuesday are auspicious for the person who is desirous of having a son. For the person who is desirous of having a daughter it is the even days after the begning of the men- strual discharge and the days of Monday, Friday, Thursday which are suitable. The days of Wednesday and Saturday are called neutral (without any sex, male or female). Therefore, the conception which is had on these days begets neutral progeny. After the beginning of the menstrual discharge the sexual in- tercourse done for having a son on the even days within 16 days is the giver of a son, and that done on the odd days is (he giver of a daughter. After this till the begmsung of the next mcnstural discharge the sexual intercourse is done meidy for the gratification of the sex- ual desire. And 15 days before her setting in the East and 5 days before her setting in the West there is her old age. Jupiter has his childhood for 15 days after his rising in the east and in the west both, and he has his old age for 15 days before his setting in the east and in the west both. DIFFERENCE OF OPINION WITH REGARD TO THE CHILDHOOD AND THE OLD AGE (OF JUPITER AND VENUS); Some Acharyas say that there arc 10 days for each of the childhood and of the old age respectively after and before the rising and set- ting of Jupiter and Venus And some have said that this time is only of 7 days. And some Acharyas have said that for the Muhurta of some extremely important work the time of these stages is only 3 days. The Moon remains a child for half day after rising and she re- mains old for 3 days before setting. Notes: The aforesaid differences seem to have come into being because of keeping the necessity of the Muhurta in mind. It should be accpeted that different Customs prevail in the different regions of the country with regard to the childhood and old age of Jupiter and Venus. Gargacharya has referred to this fact in the shloka given be- low; yRi (KdSN I | that is, before and after or on both the sides the childhood and old age of Jupiter and Venus should be regarded as 10 days in the plac- es near the Viodhyachala Mountains, 7 days in Ujjain, 6 days in Ben- gal, 5 days in Huna region and 3 days in the other provinces. f^rs Muhurta Chinta Mam ^ I ^Efg ^ f^Eft: | Re 11 28. THE MUHURTA FOR MUNDANA OR CHUDAKARANA (The first cutting of hair of a child); The doing of die Mundana or Chudakarana Samskara is auspicious in the 3 etc. odd years from the time of conception or that of birth, on the other Tithis excepting 8/ 12/4/9/14/1/6 and the day of Parva, in the Uttarayana of the Sun ex- cepting in the month of Chaitra (that is, in Magha, Phalguna, Baisakba, Jycstha and Ashadha), on the days of Wednesday, Mon- day, Thursday and Friday, in an auspicious Lagna or Ascendant and Navamsha but occpling the Lagna or Ascendant of the 8th sign from the Janama Rashi and the Janama Lagna (the birth sign and the Na- tal Ascendant) in any Lagna or Ascendant, when the 8th House from the Natal Ascendant is without any Planet, in the Nakshtras named as Mridu, Chara and Laghu alongwith the Nakshtras of Jyeshtfaa and without that of Anuradha (that is, in the Nakshtras of Mhgasbira, Rswati, Chitra, Swati, Punamsu, Shravana, Dhanishtha, Shatabhisha, Hasta, Ashwint, Pusbya and Jysshtha in these 12 Nakshtras) and when the Malefic Planets are in the 3/6/11 Houses. The Sasnsakam f^rfrr >Hlcir«?Tt>^I?l^4fl5^; 113V 11 THE MUHURTA FOR KSHORA-KARMA (making the baiter cut the beard moustache and the hair of the head). It is auspicious to clean the teeth, to get Kshora Karma1 done and to get the nails cut on the tithis, days and the Nakshtras which have been stated as proper and auspicious for the Muhurta of Mundana. But 'Kshora- Karma' etc. should be left and should not be done on the days of Saturday, Tuesday and Sunday, on the 9th day from the previous Kshora, in the evening time, on the Tithis named' as Rikta (4/9/14) on the Tithis of Parva and in the night. One who wants his well be- ing and his benefit should not clean his teeth, should not do Kshora Karma and should not cut his nails without 'aasana' (or mat) for sit- ting, during war on the battlefield, in some (other) village, on the day of travelling, after having bath, after oiling and after having taken meal. Notes; The doing of 'Kshora Karma' on the auspicious days has been said to be longevity increasing and the doing of it on inauspi- cious days is harmful for longevity. On the 9th day from the previous 'Kshora Karma' another 'Kshora Karma' should not be done. Thus if Kshora Karma was done on Wednesday, another such Karma should not be done on the 9th day from Wednesday, that is on Thursday. While doing Kshora Karma there should be the use of nig or mat etc. If there is necessity of it. Brahmins may do Kshora Karma on Sunday, Kshtriyas on Tuesday and Vaishyas and shudras on Satur- day. The consideration of time has not been said with regard to the Raaj-Karmachahs (Persons in the king's or govenunent setvkt). Muhurta Chtnta Masi qrti^ ^T^S®T ^ f^mf^ ^IW cKl^gi: ! |'^ 11 THE TIME OF UPANAYANA SAMSKARA (the cerernon>' of investiture with tl^ sacred thread or Janeu): The Upanayana Samskara of the Brahmins has been decided to be done in the 8th or the 5th year from the time of conception or of birth, that of the Kshtriyas in the 6th or the 11th year and that of the Vaishyas in the 8th or the 12th year; (This is an ideal time for the Samskara of Upanayana), If this Samskara is done when twice of the prescribed time has passed, then (his Samskara has been regarded as of medium level. Notes: The word Upanayana is a compound word made up of 'upa' which means near and 'Nayana' which means taking. Thus it means taking near the Guru or the Acharya for getting education or studying the Vedas. The idea of Upanayana is of great significance. It refers to the fact that one birth of the child took place at the home of its father and mother and another birth will take place at the home of the Guru. After the 'Upanayana the child will be referred to as 'Dwija' or the twice-born, that is a person who is bom twice 'Upanayana 1 is called Vrata-bandhana also. The main rite in the Upanayana Samskara is the investiture with the Janeu or the sacred thread. Therefore it is called Yagyopaveeta or Vratabandha Samaskara also The Samskaras of Upanayana and of Vedarambha are very near to each other so near that these can be hardly dilferentiated. The Vedarambha Samaskara (getting the edu- cation of the Vedas) begins just with the Upanayana Samaskara even today both these Samskaras are performed at the same time in the Gumkulas (Gurukula means the home of the teacher. In the ancient times, the Shi&hya or the pupil went to the home of the Guru or teacher to get education. He resided there as long as his edcuation was completed) The word Upanayana itself means that the child is now worthy of and dedicated to gel the knowledge of the Vedas. #1 Wcf | Muhurta Chtnta Mani ^T^TFTT qetl^ ^T^S®T ^ f^PTf^ 7ft17T cKI^EfT: \\$%\\ THE TIME OF UPANAYANA SAMSKARA (the ceremony of investiture with the sacred thread or Janeu): The Upanayana Samskara of the Brahmins has been decided to be done in the 8th or the 5th year from the time of conception or of birth, that of the Kshtriyas in the 6th or the 11th year and that of the Vaishyas in the 8th or (he 12th year; (This is an ideal time for the Samskara of Upanayana). If this Samskara is done when twice of the prescribed time has passed, then this Samskara has been regarded as of medium level. Notes: The word Upanayana is a compound word made up of 'upa' which means near and 'Nayana' which means taking. Thus it means taking near the Guru or the Acharya for getting education or studying the Vedas. The idea of Upanayana is of great significance. The Vedarambha Samaskara (getting the edu- cation of the Vedas) begins just with the Upanayana Samaskara even today both these Samskaras are performed at the same time in the Gumkulas (Gurukula means the home of the teacher. In the ancient times, the Shishya or the pupil went to the home of the Guru or teacher to get education. He resided there as long as his edcuation was completed) The word Upanayana itself means that the child is now worthy of and dedicated to gel the knowledge of the Vedas.

The Samsakaru ^TTf^fe^Sf^^WRT^T 11 Wo 11 THE MUHURTA FOR THE Ul'AN AY AN A SAMSKARA: It is auspicious to perform the Samskara of Upanayana in the Naksfatras named as Kshipra (Hasta, Ashwini and Pushya), those named as Dhmwa (the 3 uttaras and Rohini), that of Ashiesha, those called Chara or movable (Swati, Punarvasu, Shravana, Dhanishtha and Shatabhisha), that of Moola, those named as Mridu or Delicate (Mhgashira, Rewati, Cfaitra and Anuradha), those of Poorvaphalguni, Poorvashadha, Poorvabhadrapada and Ardra, on the 5 days of Sun- day, Wednesday, Thursday. Friday and Monday, on the Tithis of 2/ 3/3/11/12/10 and in the Shukla Paksha or the bright half of (he month. The first 3rd part (that is upto the 5th Tithi of the Krishna Paksha) of the Krishna Paksha is also acceptable The Upanayana samskara is not to be done in the 3rd part of the day or in the afternoon which is called 'Apranha 1 in Hindi. Notes: The first third part of the Dinamaana is called 'Poorvanha; the 2nd part, 'Madhyanha'; and the 3rd part; 'Apranha'. It is the rites done to the gods which are properly done in the 'Poorvanha' of the day; those done to men in the 'Madhyanha'; and those done to the manes (Shraddha etc.) in the 'Apranha'. The main period of performing Upanayana is the 'Poorvanha' of the day. The Madhyanha and the Apranha should be altogether rejected for this purpose. If there is the lack of an auspicious Ascendant in the Poorvanha, then Upanayana may be performed in the Madhyanha as well. Upanayana is extremely auspicious and proper in the month of Chaitra and at the time when (he Sun is gone in the sign of Pisces. According to Brahmanshi Vashishtha, Kashyapa and Narada the 16 Nakshtras of Hasta, Chitra, Swati. Shravana, Dhanishtha, Shatabhisha, Rohini, Mrigashira, all the three Uttaras, Anuradha, Rewati, Ashwini, Punarvasu and Pushya arc auspicious for Upanayana. These are stated to be auspicious by the Rishis (sages) that is, are 'Arsha'. The Upanayana has been said to be inauspicious for the Brahmins if it is dorss in the Nakshtia of Punarvasu also. ^nf^R^sRr^TWRT^T 11 «o 11 THE MUHURTA FOR THE UPANAYANA SAMSKARA: It is auspicious to perform the Samskara of Upanayana in the Nakshlras named as Kshipra (Hasta, Ashwini and Pushya), those named as Dhruwa (the 3 uttaras and Rohini), that of Ashlesha, those called Chara or movable (Swati, Punarvasu, Shravana, Dhanishtha and Shatabhisha), that of Moola, those named as Mridu or Delicate (Mhgashira, Rcwati, Chitra and Anuradha), those of Poorvaphalguni, Poorvashadha, Poorvabhadrapada and Ardra, on the 5 days of Sun- day, Wednesday, Thursday, Friday and Monday, on the Tithis of 2/ 3/5/11/12/10 and in the Shukla Paksha or the bright half of the month. The first 3rd part (that is upto the 5th Tithi of the Krishna Paksha) of the Krishna Paksha is also acceptable. These are stated to be auspicious by the Rishis (sages) that is, are 'Arsha'. The Upanayana has been said to be inauspicious for the Brahmins if it is done in the Nakshtra of Punarvasu also.

THE EFFECTS OF THE NAVAMSHA OF THE PLANETS THE SUN ETC IN THE LAGNA OR ASCENDANT OF UPANAYANA; If there is the Navamsha of the Sun in the Lagna' or Ascendant in which the 'Upanayana is being performed, then that child will be cruel-minded, if of the Moon he will be stupid, if of Mars he will be a sinner, if of Mercury Ik will be clever, if of Jupi- ter he will be the doer of all the 6 deeds which are worth doing (the doing of yagya or Yajna, making some one do Yagya, taking of alms, giving of alms, studying and teaching these are called 'Shatakarma'), if of Venus he will be the doer of yagya or yajna and wealthy, and if of Saturn he will be stupid. I | (^o 11 THE SPECIALITY ABOUT THE NAVAMSHA OF THE MOON: At the time of Upanayana Samskara the Moon may be in any sign, but if she is in the Navamsha of an auspicious sign, then that child remains engaged in getting education, if she is in the Navamsha of a Malefic sign, then he will be penurious, if she re- mains in ter own Navamsha, he will remain very unhappy. But if the Moon is there in the Nakshtras of Shravana and Punarvasu and then she remains in her own Navamsha, then he will tecome wealthy, 11^11 THE EFFECTS OF THE PLANETS THE SUN ETC. WHEN THEY ARE POSITED IN THE ANGLES: If in the Ascendant which is there at the time of doing the 'Upanayana Samskara' the Sun is situated in an angle, the child whose Upanayana is done joins royal or government service; if the Moon is in an angle he becomes a trad- er or a merchant; if Mars, a trader of arms & weapons (or gets job in the army); if Mercury, a teacher; if Jupiter, an excellent scholar or a learned man; if Venus, wealthy; and if Saturn is placed in an Angle he becomes a servant to the Mlechhas or the low born dirty men. ANADHYAYA (that is, the Tithis which should be rejected): The 10th Tithi of the Shukia Paksha or bright half of the month of Ashadha, the 2nd Tithi of the Shukia Paksha of Jyeshtha, the 11th Tithi of the Shukia Paksha of Pausha, the I2th Tithi of the Shukia Paksha of Magha and the 1/8/14/15/30 Tithis of the Shukia and Krishna both Pakshas of every month and the day of Sankranti, these (Tithis and days) are named as 'Anadhyaya' in the doing of the 'Upanayana Samskara' that is, the 'Upanayana Samskara' should not be done on these. The Sunukitrxs THE MUHURTA FOR THE KESHANTA KARMA AND S AMA V ART ANA SAMS KARA. (Gctlihg the hair cut and the com- pletion of Brahmacharja): In the Muhurta which has been told as aus- picious for Mundana (Stated in the 29th Shloka of this very chapter) Samskara, the doing of 'the Keshanta Karma' is auspicious. This Smaskara is performed in the !6th year. And the Muhurta which has been told as auspicious for the Upanayana Samskara is also auspi- cious for the 'Samavartana Karma* or the completion of Brahmachaiya. Notes: The 'Keshanta Samskara' of a Brahmin should be done in the 16th year, of a Kshlrtya in the 22nd year and of a Vaishya in the 24th year. After completing the study of the Vedas and the Vedangas at the house of the Guru or teacher there used to be for the Brahamachans 'Samavartana Samskara' before entering into or tak- ing the Grihastha Ashrama. In the modern times tie getting of de- gree after graduation is also a kind of Samarvatana Samskara or con- vocation. Our Rishis divided the whole life of man, taking it as consis- ting of 100 years, into 4 Ashramas-tfeg Brahamacharyashrama, tfee Grahasthashrama the Vanaprasthashrama and the Sanyasashrama-cach of which spread over 25 years of man's life. There an. Samskaras at the different stages of life in each of these Ashramas. The Braharaacharyashraina is tea first of these Ashramas. In this Ashrama one coinpfcted his education. He resided in Gurukula or the Home of the Gum (teacher). The Sunsakaras THE MUHURTA FOR THE KESHANTA KARMA AND SAMAVARTANA SAMSKARA: (Getting the hair cut and the com- pletion of Brahmacharya): In the Muhurta which has been told as aus- picious for Mundana (Stated in the 29th Shloka of this very chapter) Samskara, the doing of 'the Keshanta Karma' is auspicious. This Smaskara is performed in the 16th year. And the Muhurta which has been told as auspicious for the Upanayana Samskara is also auspi- cious for the 'Samavartana Karma* or the completion of Brahmacharya. Notes: The 'Keshanta Samskara' of a Brahmin should be done in the 16th year, of a Kshlriya in the 22nd year and of a Vaisbya in the 24th year. After completing the study of the Vedas and the Vedangas at the house of the Guru or teacher there used to be for the Brahamacharis 'Samavartana Samskara' before entering into or tak- ing the Grihastha Ashranta. In the modern times tie getting of de- gree after graduation is also a kind of Samarvatana Samskara or con- vocation. Our Rishis divided the whole life of man, taking it as consis- ting of 100 years, into 4 Ashramas-the Brahamacharyashrama, the Grahasthashrama, the Vanaprasthashrama nnd the Sanyasashrama-each of which spread over 25 years of man's life. There are Samskaras at the different stages of life in each of these Ashramas. The Brahamacharyashrama is the first of these Ashramas. In this Ashrama one completed his education. He resided in Gunikula or the Home of the Guru (teacher).
